. The inductive assumption simply reflects the fact that the parameter updates are coupled, and it suffices to verify that, starting from a valid initialization, the stated conclusions are preserved from one iteration to the next.

Lemma C.3

(Restatement of Lemma
C.2
, part I)
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
4.5
, there exist a time dependent scalars
C_{1}^{(t)}
such that for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)}=C_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}.

In addition,
C_{1}^{(t)}
follows the iterative rules as

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-C_{1}^{(t)}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg].

Here, for each
k\in[8]
,
F_{k,\sigma}(\cdot)
is continuously differentiable with respect to its argument, where
\sigma
is treated as a fixed constant.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{V}_{21}=C_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\mathbf{W}_{21}=-C_{2}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
, then by Lemma
C.1
, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{21}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(\mathbf{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}^{(t)})=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg(\frac{\alpha}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\bigg)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha}{n}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{1}}+C_{1}^{(t)}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{2}}.

In the next, we analyze the value of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
respectively. For any given
\bm{\theta}_{*}
and
\bm{\theta}_{0}
, let
\mathbf{A}
be an orthogonal matrix defined as

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}=\bigg[\bm{\theta}_{*},\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}},\bm{\xi}_{3},\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{d}\bigg]\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

where
\bm{\xi}_{3}\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{d}
are normalized vectors orthogonal to
\bm{\theta}_{*}
and
\bm{\theta}_{0}
(For notational consistency, we will use
\bm{\xi}_{1}
and
\bm{\xi}_{2}
to represent
\bm{\theta}_{*}
and
\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}}
, respectively, in certain summation contexts.). In addition, We define that
\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}=\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}
, which implies that
y_{i}=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i,1})
. We further denote that
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}=y_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}
. Then by Lemma
F.22
, we know that all coordinates of
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}
are independent with each other. The first coordinate
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}
follows a folded normal distribution, and other coordinates still follow normal distributions. Notice that we can re-write
\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\mathbf{z}_{i}\rangle=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}+\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}
, which implies that both
e^{-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\mathbf{z}_{i}\rangle}
and
\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}
are all independent with the coordinates from
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,3}
to
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,d}
. In addition, Lemma
F.21
guarantees that
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}
are independent with
\bm{\theta}_{*}
,
\bm{\theta}_{0}
, and
\bm{\xi}_{3},\ldots,\bm{\xi}_{d}
.
Based on all these preliminaries, we calculate
I_{1}
as

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\Bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},1}\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{1,1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg]}_{I_{1,2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{1,3}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{d}\sum_{j_{2}\neq j_{1}}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j_{2}}\bm{\xi}_{j_{1}}\bm{\xi}_{j_{2}}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{1,4}}.

(C.2)

Through the independence stated above, and the calculations demonstrated in Section
F.1
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1,1}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},1}\Bigg]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top}\big]=\frac{1}{d}\bigg(\frac{2n}{\pi}\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}+f_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the expectation of the first term is derived in Lemma
F.3
, with
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
being two analytic functions of
C_{2}^{(t)}
and
\sigma
, and
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top}]=\mathbf{I}_{d}/d
as demonstrated in Lemma
F.20
.
Similarly, we also have

\displaystyle I_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\Bigg]\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\bigg(nC_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}+f_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the expectation of the first term is derived in Lemma
F.1
. For
I_{1,3}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1,3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\big]+\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}\neq i_{1}}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\big]=\frac{d-2}{d}\bigg(\sigma^{2}e^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{f_{5}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{6}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the second equation holds as
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}]=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}]=0
, and the expectation of the last equation is given in Lemma
F.5
. Lastly, for the term
I_{1,4}
, by the independence among these random variables and the fact that each
\bm{\xi}_{j}
is symmetric, we have

\displaystyle I_{1,4}=\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{d}\sum_{j_{2}\neq j_{1}}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j_{2}}\big]\mathbb{E}[\bm{\xi}_{j_{1}}]\mathbb{E}[\bm{\xi}_{j_{2}}^{\top}]=\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}.

Combining these results into (
C
), we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{1}=\frac{n\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{d}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\mathbf{I}_{d}.

(C.3)

Similarly, we can also separate
I_{2}
as

\displaystyle I_{2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\Bigg]=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},1}\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{2,1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}\Bigg]}_{I_{2,2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{2,3}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{d}\sum_{j_{2}\neq j_{1}}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j_{1}}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j_{2}}\bm{\xi}_{j_{1}}\bm{\xi}_{j_{2}}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{2,4}}.

(C.4)

We calculate each term following a similar procedure. For
I_{2,1}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2,1}=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},1}\Bigg]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top}\big]=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+\frac{f_{7}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{8}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the expectation is provided in Lemma
F.4
. For
I_{2,2}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2,2}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\Bigg]\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg([C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}+f_{9}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{10}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the expectation is provided in Lemma
F.2
. For
I_{2,3}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2,3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\big]+\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}\neq i_{1}}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j}\big]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=3}^{d}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\big[(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)})^{2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}\big]=\frac{(d-2)\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(e^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{f_{11}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{12}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}\bigg)\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d},

where the expectation is provided in Lemma
F.6
. In addition,
I_{2,4}=\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}
by the symmetry of
\bm{\xi}_{j}
. Substituting these results into (
C
), we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{2}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\mathbf{I}_{d}.

(C.5)

Hence, we prove the induction that by assuming at
t
-th iteration,
\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)}=C_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\mathbf{W}_{12}^{(t)}=-C_{2}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
, the gradient
\nabla_{\mathbf{V}_{21}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(\mathbf{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
is also proportional to the identity matrix
\mathbf{I}_{d}
. In addition, (
C.3
) and (
C.5
) establish the iterative rule for the coefficient
C_{1}^{(t)}
as

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-C_{1}^{(t)}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg].

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.4

(Restatement of Lemma
C.2
, part II)
.

Under the same conditions of Theorem
4.5
, there exist a time dependent scalar
C_{2}^{(t)}
such that for
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{12}^{(t)}=-C_{2}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}.

In addition,
C_{2}^{(t)}
follows the iterative rules as

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta C_{1}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-C_{1}^{(t)}C_{2}^{(t)}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg].

Here, for each
k\in[8]
,
F_{k,\sigma}(\cdot)
is continuously differentiable with respect to its argument, where
\sigma
is treated as a fixed constant.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{V}_{21}=C_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\mathbf{W}_{21}=-C_{2}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
, then by Lemma
C.1
, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{12}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(\mathbf{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{context}}\Big({\rm diag}(\mathbf{s}^{(t)})\!-\!\mathbf{s}^{(t)}(\mathbf{s}^{(t)})^{\top}\!\Big)\mathbf{Z}_{\mathrm{context}}^{\top}(\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)})^{\top}\!\bigg(\frac{\alpha}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{z}_{i}\!-\!\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{z}_{i}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\!\bigg)\bm{\theta}_{0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\alpha C_{1}^{(t)}}{n}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}-\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{3}}

\displaystyle+C_{1}^{2}(t)\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}-\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]}_{I_{4}}

Utilizing the same definition of the orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{A}
,
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}
for all
i\in[n]
, and
\bm{\xi}_{j}
for all
j\in[d]
as in the proof of Lemma
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}(\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}-\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2}})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{2}\bm{\xi}_{2}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j\neq 2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}.

(C.6)

Here the second term
\mathbb{E}\big[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j\neq 2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\big]
because for any
j\neq 2
, we have
\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\big]\mathbb{E}[\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}]=\mathbf{0}
through a similar argument in Lemma
C.3
. Then we get (
C
) by plugging the definition that
\bm{\xi}_{2}=\frac{(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}}{\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}}
, and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\xi}_{j}^{\top}]=\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d}
. This demonstrates that
I_{3}
is always proportional to
\mathbf{I}_{d}
. In the next, we calculate the coefficient in (
C
). We first separate the term
\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\big]
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}\Big]}_{I_{3,1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\Big]}_{I_{3,2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}\Big]}_{I_{3,3}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}\Big]}_{I_{3,4}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\Big]}_{I_{3,5}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}\Big]}_{I_{3,6}}.

(C.7)

We carefully calculate these terms trough the lemmas provided in Appendix
F.1
, respectively. For the term
I_{3,1}
, by Lemma
F.8
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,1}=\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}nC_{2}^{(t)}+f_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d},

where
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
are two analytic functions of
C_{2}^{(t)}
and
\sigma
.
For the term
I_{3,2}
, by Lemma
F.7
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,2}=\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}n\big(\sigma^{2}[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}+1\big)+f_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{3,3}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d\big(1+C_{2}^{(t)}\big)\sigma^{4}e^{(2C_{2}^{(t)}+1)\sigma^{2}/2}+f_{5}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{6}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

The second equation holds as by the independence between
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}
when
i_{1}\neq i
, and the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}]=\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}]=0
. The last equation is calculated based on Lemma
F.9
. For the term
I_{3,4}
, by Lemma
F.11
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,4}=\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}nC_{2}^{(t)}+f_{7}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{8}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{3,5}
, by Lemma
F.10
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,5}=\sigma^{6}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}n[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}+f_{9}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{10}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{3,6}
, similar to the procedure of calculation for
I_{3,3}
and utilizing the result of Lemma
F.12
, we have

\displaystyle I_{3,6}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}e^{-\langle\mathbf{z}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d\sigma^{4}e^{(2C_{2}^{(t)}+1)\sigma^{2}/2}C_{2}^{(t)}+f_{11}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)+\frac{nf_{12}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

Plugging all these results into (
C
), we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{3}=\frac{n\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{d}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\mathbf{I}_{d}.

(C.8)

In the next, we consider calculating
I_{4}
through a similar procedure as

\displaystyle I_{4}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}(\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}-\mathbf{z}_{i_{2}})\mathbf{z}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{A}^{\top}\mathbf{z}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\mathbf{A}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2}})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},j})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\bm{\xi}_{j}\bm{\theta}_{0}^{\top}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\Big]\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}.

This result demonstrates that
I_{4}
is also proportional to
\mathbf{I}_{d}
. For the coefficient

\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\big]
, we separate it as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}(\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}-\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2})\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1}}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}\Big]}_{I_{4,1}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\Big]}_{I_{4,2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}\Big]}_{I_{4,3}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},1}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,1}\Big]}_{I_{4,4}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\Big]}_{I_{4,5}}

\displaystyle-\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{2},2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i_{1},j}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}\Big]}_{I_{4,6}}.

(C.9)

We carefully calculate these terms trough the lemmas provided in Appendix
F.1
, respectively. For the term
I_{4,1}
, by Lemma
F.14
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,1}=\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma^{4}C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{f_{13}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{14}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{4,2}
, by Lemma
F.13
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,2}=C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}\big(\sigma^{2}[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}+1\big)+\frac{f_{15}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{16}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{4,3}
, by Lemma
F.15
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,3}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\big(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\big)^{2}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2dC_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}e^{\sigma^{2}[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}}}{n}+\frac{f_{17}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{18}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{4,4}
, by Lemma
F.17
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,4}=\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma^{4}C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{f_{19}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{20}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{4,5}
, by Lemma
F.16
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,5}=C_{2}^{3}(t)\sigma^{6}+\frac{f_{21}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{22}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}.

For the term
I_{4,6}
, similar to the procedure of calculation for
I_{4,3}
and utilizing the result of Lemma
F.18
, we have

\displaystyle I_{4,6}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{d}\mathbb{E}\big[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\big(\mathbf{s}_{i}^{(t)}\big)^{2}\mathbf{s}_{i_{2}}^{(t)}\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,2}\big]\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{\mathbf{z}}_{i,j}^{2}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{dC_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}e^{\sigma^{2}[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}}}{n}+\frac{f_{23}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{24}(C_{2}^{(t)},\sigma)}{d}

Plugging all these results into (
C
), we obtain that

\displaystyle I_{4}=\frac{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}}{d}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\mathbf{I}_{d}.

(C.10)

Therefore, we prove the induction that by assuming at
t
-th iteration,
\mathbf{V}_{21}^{(t)}=C_{1}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\mathbf{W}_{12}^{(t)}=-C_{2}^{(t)}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}
, the gradient
\nabla_{\mathbf{W}_{12}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(\mathbf{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}^{(t)})
is also proportional to the identity matrix
\mathbf{I}_{d}
. In addition, (
C.8
) and (
C.10
) establish the iterative rule for the coefficient
C_{1}^{(t)}
as

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta C_{1}^{(t)}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{C_{2}^{(t)}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-C_{1}^{(t)}C_{2}^{(t)}\bigg(1+\frac{de^{[C_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg].

This completes the proof.
∎

Therefore, we have shown that throughout training, the parameter matrices

\mathbf{V}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{W}^{(t)}
always remain in the structured parameterization
defined in Lemma
4.4
. Consequently, for our learning task,
analyzing the training loss
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})
along the
iterates
\mathbf{V}^{(t)},\mathbf{W}^{(t)}
is equivalent to studying a reduced
two-dimensional proxy loss

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
with respect to the coefficients

C_{1}^{(t)}
and
C_{2}^{(t)}
. Specifically, the proxy loss
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
is defined as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2}):=\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}\!\bigg(\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}\\
C_{1}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix},\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&-C_{2}\cdot\mathbf{I}_{d}\\
\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}&\mathbf{0}_{d\times d}\end{bmatrix}\bigg).

Under this parameterization, the update rules for
C_{1}^{(t)}
and
C_{2}^{(t)}

given in (
C.2
) correspond exactly to gradient descent applied
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
with zero initialization.
From (
C.2
), we further derive that

\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
admits the following explicit form:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\frac{C_{1}^{2}}{2}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg]+c,

where
c
is a constant independent of
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.
And we can immediately conclude that
F_{1,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})=F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2}),F_{2,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})=F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2}),F_{3,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})=F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2}),F_{4,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})=F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2})
.
In addition, we define that

\displaystyle G_{1}(C_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d};

\displaystyle G_{2}(C_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d};

\displaystyle G_{3}(C_{2})=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{de^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d};

\displaystyle G_{4}(C_{2})=

\displaystyle 1+\frac{de^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}.

(C.11)

With these notations, we prove the first conclusion in Theorem
4.5
, which states that there exists an invariant compact set
\mathcal{R}=[0,2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}]\times[0,2]
for
\mathbf{C}^{(t)}=[C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)}]^{\top}
during the training.

Lemma C.5

(First conclusion in Theorem
4.5
)
.

Consider the iterative rules for
\mathbf{C}^{(t)}=[C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)}]^{\top}
given in (
C.2
) with initializations
C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)}=0
. Suppose that
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{n}\big)
,
\alpha\leq\mathcal{O}(1)
and
n\geq\Omega(d^{2})
, then it holds that:

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2};\ 0\leq C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 2,

(C.12)

for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

We prove this lemma by induction. It’s evident that (
C.12
) holds at
t=0
. In the next, we assume it holds at
t
-th iteration and attempt to prove it still holds at
t+1
-th iteration. Since (
C.12
) holds, there exists
K\leq O(1)
such that
|F_{i,\sigma}(C_{2}^{(t)})|,e^{4\sigma^{2}}\leq\frac{K}{8}
for all
i\in[8]
. Consequently, combined with the condition that
n\geq\Omega(d^{2})
, we can directly obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\frac{3}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d};\quad\frac{2}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d};

\displaystyle 1-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq 1+\frac{K}{d};\quad 1-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq 1+\frac{K}{d};

\displaystyle\frac{3}{5}\leq\frac{\frac{3}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}}{\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}}\leq\frac{G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})}\leq\frac{\frac{9}{4\pi}+\frac{K}{d}}{\frac{17}{8\pi}-\frac{K}{d}}\leq\frac{3}{2};\quad 1-\frac{K}{d}\leq\frac{G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})}\leq 1+\frac{K}{d};\quad\frac{G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})}\leq 2.

We first prove that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq 0
from two cases: (1).
C_{1}^{(t)}\leq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}
; and (2).
C_{1}^{(t)}>\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}
. If
C_{1}^{(t)}\leq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}\frac{5}{3}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq 0,

which implies that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq C_{1}^{(t)}\geq 0
. If
C_{1}^{(t)}>\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}
, then we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq-2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\bigg(\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg),

where we replace
C_{1}^{(t)}
and
G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})
with their upper bounds respectively. Since
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{n}\big)
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{5}-\frac{2\eta\sigma^{2}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg)\geq 0.

The last inequality holds as
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{n}\big)
is sufficiently small.
This proves that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq 0
. Similarly, to prove
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
, we also consider two cases: (1).
C_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}
; and (2).
C_{1}^{(t)}<\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}
. For the first case where
C_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq 0,

which implies that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\leq C_{1}^{(t)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
. On the other hand where
C_{1}^{(t)}<\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}
, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\bigg(\frac{3}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg).

This can further implies that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}+\eta\Big(\alpha e^{1/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)\leq\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{3}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg)\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}.

This completes the proof that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
. In the following, we proceed with
C_{2}^{(t)}
with the similar techniques. We first prove that
C_{2}^{(t+1)}\geq 0
under (1).
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{4}
; and (2).
C_{2}^{(t)}>\frac{1}{4}
. For the first case where
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{4}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}^{(t)}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{2}(t)C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq C_{1}^{(t)}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-4\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\frac{1}{4}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)\geq 0,

which implies that
C_{2}^{(t+1)}\geq C_{2}^{(t)}\geq 0
. On the other hand when
C_{2}^{(t)}>\frac{1}{4}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}^{(t)}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{2}(t)C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq

\displaystyle-C_{1}^{2}(t)C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq-8\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}},

where the last inequality holds as we substitute the upper bounds that
C_{1}^{(t)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
and
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 2
. This result helps us further derive that

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t+1)}=

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{\sigma^{4}\eta}{d}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}^{(t)}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{2}(t)C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)\geq\frac{1}{4}-\frac{8\eta\sigma^{4}\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{d}\geq 0.

This completes the proof that
C_{2}^{(t+1)}\geq 0
. In the next, we prove that
C_{2}^{(t)}
is always smaller than
2
. This would be a little tricky, and we consider two different phases. We first prove that there exists an iteration
T_{1}
, which serves the first time such that
C_{1}
reaches
\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}
. We prove that for all
t\leq T_{1}
, it holds that
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 1
by induction. Notice that for any
t\leq T_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\Delta C_{1}^{(t)}=

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}-C_{1}^{(t)}=\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}\frac{3d+3\pi K/4}{3d-\pi K}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\bigg)\geq\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{4d}\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)}).

On the other hand, we can upper bound the increments of
C_{2}^{(t)}
as

\displaystyle\Delta C_{2}^{(t)}=

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t+1)}-C_{2}^{(t)}=\frac{\eta\sigma^{4}}{d}\bigg(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}^{(t)}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{2}(t)C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta\sigma^{4}}{d}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}C_{1}^{(t)}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq\frac{2\eta\sigma^{4}}{d}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}.

This implies that for all
t\leq T_{1}
,
\frac{\Delta C_{2}^{(t)}}{\Delta C_{1}^{(t)}}\leq\frac{8\alpha\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}
. Consequently, we obtain that for all
t\leq T_{1}
,
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{8\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}(3d-\pi K)^{2}}{(4d+\pi K)^{2}}\leq 1
, which holds as
\sigma^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}
and
\alpha\leq 1
. Next, we prove for the case when
t\geq T_{1}
. We first prove that
C_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta
for any
t\geq T_{1}
by induction. When
\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta\leq C_{1}^{(t)}\leq\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}\frac{4d+\pi K}{3d-\pi K}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq 0.

This implies that
C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq C_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta
. When
C_{1}^{(t)}>\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\geq-2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\bigg(\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg).

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t+1)}\geq

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{2}}{d}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{1}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}G_{2}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\frac{2\eta\sigma^{2}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}\bigg)\geq\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta.

Now, by establishing the fact
C_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta
, we can follow the previous proof techniques by induction. To prove that
C_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq 2
, we also consider two cases: (1).
C_{2}^{(t)}\geq\frac{19}{10}
; and (2).
C_{2}^{(t)}<\frac{19}{10}
. For the first case where
C_{2}^{(t)}\geq\frac{19}{10}
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\bigg(\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}(3d-\pi K)}{4d+\pi K}-\eta\bigg)\frac{19}{10}\frac{G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})}{1+\frac{K}{d}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})+2\eta G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\frac{\frac{17d}{10}-(4+2\pi)K}{(4d+\pi K)(1+\frac{K}{d})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-\frac{3\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{8}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq 0,

where the last inequality holds as
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{n}\big)
is sufficiently small.
This implies that
C_{2}^{(t+1)}\leq C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 2
. In addition, when
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq\frac{19}{10}
, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}^{(t)}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)\leq C_{1}^{(t)}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})\leq 4\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}.

Consequently, we can obtain that

\displaystyle C_{2}^{(t+1)}=C_{2}^{(t)}+\frac{\eta\sigma^{4}}{d}C_{1}^{(t)}\Big(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}G_{3}(C_{2}^{(t)})-C_{1}^{(t)}C_{2}^{(t)}G_{4}(C_{2}^{(t)})\Big)\leq\frac{19}{10}+\frac{4\eta\sigma^{4}\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{d}\leq 2,

where the last inequality holds as
\eta\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{n}\big)
is sufficiently small. This completes the proof that
C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 2
for all
t\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

We have demonstrated in Lemma
C.5
that the iterates of
C_{1}^{(t)}
and
C_{2}^{(t)}
always stay inner the region
\mathcal{R}=[0,2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}]\times[0,2]
. We next prove that within this region, there exists a unique local minimum
(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
of the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
. We first introduce Newton–Kantorovich Theorem, which helps demonstrate our results.

Theorem C.6

(Newton–Kantorovich Theorem, cf. Theorem 5.5.1 in
32
)
.

Let
F:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a continuously differentiable function.
Suppose there exists a point
\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and constants
\beta,\gamma,L
such that:

1.

F(\cdot)
is differentiable at
\mathbf{x}_{0}
, and
F^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{0})
is invertible, satisfying that

\displaystyle\|F^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{0})^{-1}\|\leq\beta;\quad\|F^{\prime}(\mathbf{x}_{0})^{-1}F(\mathbf{x}_{0})\|\leq\gamma.

2.

F^{\prime}(\cdot)
is Lipschitz continuous with constant
\iota
in a neighborhood of
\mathbf{x}_{0}
radius
\bar{r}
satisfying that

\displaystyle\bar{r}\geq r_{-}=\frac{1-\sqrt{1-2\beta\gamma\iota}}{\beta\iota}

3.

The constants
\beta,\gamma,\iota
satisfy that
\beta\gamma\iota\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

Then there exists a unique fixed point
\mathbf{x}^{*}
of
F(\cdot)
in the neighborhood of
\mathbf{x}_{0}
with radius equal to
\max\{\bar{r},\frac{1+\sqrt{1-2\beta\gamma\iota}}{\beta\iota}\}
. In addition,
\mathbf{x}^{*}
satisfies that
\|\mathbf{x}^{*}-\mathbf{x}_{0}\|\leq r_{-}
.

Then the following Lemma
C.7
demonstrates that there exists a unique local minimum of the proxy loss
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
by utilizing the Newton-Kantorovich Theorem
C.6
.

Lemma C.7

(Second conclusion in Theorem
4.5
)
.

Let
\mathcal{R}:=[0,2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}]\times[0,2]
. Then there exists a unique local minimum
\mathbf{C}^{*}=[C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*}]^{\top}
of the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
inner
\mathcal{R}
, and this local minimum satisfies that

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}^{*}-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big|,\big|C_{2}^{*}-1\big|\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{1}{d}\bigg).

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

We prove this lemma by demonstrating that
\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
only has one unique fixed point by Newton–Kantorovich Theorem
C.6
. Since Newton–Kantorovich Theorem
C.6
does not require a particular norm, we specify the
\ell_{\infty}
norm
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
in our proof.
We first calculate the Hessian matrix of
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
as

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}^{2}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}\partial C_{2}}=-\frac{\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{1,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{2,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-2C_{1}C_{2}\bigg(1+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{3,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{4,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}\partial C_{1}}=-\frac{\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(1+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-2C_{1}C_{2}\bigg(1+\frac{d}{n}e^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}^{2}}=-\frac{C_{1}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg[\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}de^{C_{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{5,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{6,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle-C_{1}\bigg(1+\frac{(1+2C_{2}^{2})de^{C_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}+\frac{F_{7,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{n}+\frac{F_{8,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})}{d}\bigg)\Bigg].

In addition, since we have
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\mathcal{R}=[0,2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}]\times[0,2]
, which is a compact set. Similar to the proof of Lemma
C.5
, we can find
K\leq\mathcal{O}(1)
, such that
\max\{|F_{k,\sigma}(C_{2})|,|F_{k,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})|,9e^{4\sigma^{2}}\}\leq\frac{K}{8}

for all
k\in[8]
.
To check the conditions of Newton-Kantorovich Theorem, we let
(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})=(\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2},1)
. Then we can calculate that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}^{2}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}+\sigma^{2}\bigg)\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}\partial C_{2}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}-\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}\partial C_{1}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}-\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}}

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}^{2}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}-\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{4}}{d}\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}}.

From which we can calculate that
\det\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big]\geq\frac{2\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{6}}{\pi d^{2}}-\frac{5K}{d^{3}}
. Consequently, we can further calculate that

\displaystyle\big\|[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})]^{-1}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max\big\{\big|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})}{\partial C_{2}^{2}}\big|,\big|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})}{\partial C_{1}^{2}}\big|\big\}+\big|\frac{\partial^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})}{\partial C_{1}\partial C_{2}}\big|}{\det\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\pi\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{2}+\pi\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{2}+2+\pi\sigma^{2})d}{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{4}}=\beta,

which completes the first condition of Newton-Kantorovich Theorem
C.6
.
In the next, we can further calculate that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}};\quad\Bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}\bigg|_{C_{1}=\widetilde{C}_{1},C_{2}=\widetilde{C}_{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{K}{d^{2}}.

Consequently, we have that

\displaystyle\big\|[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})]^{-1}\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})]^{-1}\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\pi\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{2}+\pi\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{2}+2+\pi\sigma^{2})K}{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{4}d}=\gamma.

In addition, for all
(C_{1},C_{2})\in\mathcal{R}
, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{16\alpha^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{\sigma^{2}}+9\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{4}+4\sigma^{4}}{d}=\iota,

implying that
\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
is Lipschitz continuous with the constant
\iota
. And we can check that
\beta\gamma\iota=\mathcal{O}(\frac{1}{d})\leq\frac{1}{2}
. For now, we have verified that all conditions of Theorem
C.6
hold. Therefore, there exists a unique fixed point
(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
of
\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
inner
\mathcal{R}
, and satisfying that

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}^{*}-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big|,\big|C_{2}^{*}-1\big|\leq r_{-}\leq\gamma\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{1}{d}\bigg).

In the next, we prove that this unique fixed point
(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
is a local minimum. Since
|C_{1}^{*}-\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}|,|C_{2}^{*}-1|\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)
, we can easily obtain that
\big\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\big)
. Consequently, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\det\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\big]\geq

\displaystyle\det\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big]-2\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle-2\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\|_{\infty}\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{6}}{\pi d^{2}}-\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{1}{d^{3}}\bigg)>0.

In addition, we can also check that

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\big]\geq

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\big[\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\big]-2\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})-\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(\widetilde{C}_{1},\widetilde{C}_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\sigma^{4}+2\sigma^{2}/\pi+\sigma^{4}}{d}-\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{1}{d^{2}}\bigg)>0.

These two results demonstrate that
\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
is strictly positive definite. Therefore, the fixed point
\mathbf{C}^{*}=[C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*}]^{\top}
of the gradient
\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
is local minimum of the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
, which completes the proof.
∎

For now, we have completed the first and second conclusions in Theorem
4.5
. In the next lemma, we demonstrate that inside the region
\mathcal{R}
, the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
follows a Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) condition, which serves as a critical step for final linear convergence rate.

Lemma C.8

(PL condition inside
\mathcal{R}
)
.

There exists a strictly positive constant
\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}
solely depending on
\alpha
and
\sigma
, such that for all
[C_{1},C_{2}]^{\top}
inner
\mathcal{R}
, the loss function
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
satisfies the following Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) condition

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\big\|_{2}^{2}\geq\frac{2\mu_{\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\big).

Proof of Lemma
C.8
.

To prove this lemma, we first introduce the nullcline of
C_{1}
in the gradient regarding
C_{1}
. By setting
[\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})]_{1}=0
, we can derive the nullcline of
C_{1}
as
G_{*}(C_{2})=\frac{\alpha e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}G_{1}(C_{2})}{G_{2}(C_{2})}
, and define that
\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})=C_{1}-G_{*}(C_{2})
. Then we can decompose the loss
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\Bigg[\frac{\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})}{2}-\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}G_{1}^{2}(C_{2})}{2G_{2}(C_{2})}\Bigg]+c,

where
c
is a constants irrelevant with
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
.
Since
(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
is the fixed point of
\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
, we can obtain that
\Delta(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})=0
, which helps us to further derive that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})=\frac{\sigma^{2}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})}{2d}+\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*}),

(C.13)

where
\psi(C_{2})=-\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}G_{1}^{2}(C_{2})}{2dG_{2}(C_{2})}
. In addition, since

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})}{\partial C_{2}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}\Delta(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})}{2d}\bigg(2\frac{\partial\Delta(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})}{\partial C_{2}}G_{2}(C_{2}^{*})+\Delta(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})G^{\prime}_{2}(C_{2}^{*})\bigg)+\psi^{\prime}(C_{2}^{*})=0,

we can immediately conclude that
\psi^{\prime}(C_{2}^{*})=0
. In addition, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{2dG^{3}_{2}(C_{2})}\Bigg[4G_{1}(C_{2})G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})+G^{2}_{1}(C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})G_{2}^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})

\displaystyle-2G_{2}^{2}(C_{2})\Big(G_{1}(C_{2})G_{1}^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})+\big[G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}\Big)-2G_{1}^{2}(C_{2})\big[G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{2dG^{3}_{2}(C_{2})}\widetilde{G}(C_{2}).

(C.14)

Similar to the previous proof of Lemma
C.5
and Lemma
C.7
, we can find
K\leq\mathcal{O}(1)
such that
\max\{|F_{k,\sigma}(C_{2})|,|F_{k,\sigma}^{\prime}(C_{2})|,9e^{4\sigma^{2}}\}\leq\frac{K}{8}
.
For the terms
G_{1}(C_{2})
and
G_{2}(C_{2})
and their derivatives, we can obtain their uniform upper and lower bounds for
C_{2}
in
[0,2]
as

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{1}(C_{2})\leq\frac{3}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d};\quad\frac{2}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}\leq G_{2}(C_{2})\leq\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}

\displaystyle|G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})-\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{K}{d};\quad|G_{1}^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})|\leq\frac{K}{d};\quad|G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})-2\sigma^{2}C_{2}|\leq\frac{K}{d};\quad|G_{2}^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})-2\sigma^{2}|\leq\frac{K}{d}.

(C.15)

This further implies that
\frac{1}{2}\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\leq\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
.
Substituting these terms into the
\widetilde{G}(C_{2})
defined in (
C
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\widetilde{G}(C_{2})-\bigg(\frac{16\sigma^{2}}{\pi^{3}}-\frac{8\sigma^{4}}{\pi^{2}}+\frac{48\sigma^{4}}{\pi^{2}}C_{2}-\frac{24\sigma^{4}}{\pi^{2}}C_{2}^{2}+\frac{12\sigma^{6}}{\pi}C_{2}^{2}-\frac{8\sigma^{6}}{\pi}C_{2}^{3}\bigg)\Bigg|\leq\frac{90K}{\pi^{3}d}.

(C.16)

By substituting the facts that
0\leq C_{2}\leq 2
inner
\mathcal{R}
, and
\sigma^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2\pi}
into (
C.16
), we can derive the lower and upper bounds for
\widetilde{G}(C_{2})
as

\displaystyle\frac{8\sigma^{2}}{\pi^{3}}\leq\widetilde{G}(C_{2})\leq\frac{29\sigma^{2}}{\pi^{3}}.

For now, we substitute the previously derived upper and lower bounds on
\widetilde{G}(C_{2})
, together with the corresponding bounds on
G_{2}(C_{2})
that
\frac{2}{\pi}-\frac{K}{d}\leq\frac{4}{\pi}+\frac{K}{d}
as established in the proof of Lemma
C.5
, into (
C
). This yields the following bounds of
\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})
uniformly for all
C_{2}
in
\mathcal{R}
as

\displaystyle\frac{4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{65d}\leq\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})\leq\frac{3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{d}.

(C.17)

Consequently, by utilizing Taylor’s expansion, the fact
\psi^{\prime}(C_{2}^{*})=0
, and the uniform upper and lower bounds for
\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})=\psi^{\prime}(C_{2}^{*})\big(C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big)+\frac{\psi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{C}_{2})}{2}\big(C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big)^{2}\leq\frac{3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{2d}\big(C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big)^{2},

(C.18)

where
\bar{C}_{2}
is an intermediate value between
C_{2}
and
C_{2}^{*}
. On the other hand, we can also have

\displaystyle\big|\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big|=\Big|\psi^{\prime}(C_{2}^{*})+\psi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{C}_{2})\big(C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big)\Big|\geq\frac{4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{65d}\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|.

(C.19)

By square on both sides of (
C.19
) and compare it with (
C.18
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\big[\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}\geq\frac{\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{600d}\big(\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})\big),

(C.20)

which establishes a PL condition for
\psi(C_{2})
. In the next, we prove that this can imply a PL condition for
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
. We first consider two components
\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}
and
\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}
of
\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\|_{2}
as following:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}=\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}G_{2}(C_{2})\Delta(C_{1},C_{2});

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}=\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big[G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})-G_{1}(C_{2})G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]}{G_{2}(C_{1},C_{2})}\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2d}G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2}).

By utilizing the results in (
C
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}\bigg|^{2}\geq

\displaystyle\frac{17\sigma^{4}}{\pi^{2}d^{2}}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2}),

(C.21)

and

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big[G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})-G_{1}(C_{2})G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]}{G_{2}(C_{2})}\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2d}G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big[\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}-\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{d}\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big[G_{1}^{\prime}(C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})-G_{1}(C_{2})G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]}{G_{2}(C_{2})}\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2d}G_{2}^{\prime}(C_{2})\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})\bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\big[\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}-\frac{5\alpha\sigma^{6}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{d^{2}}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2}),

(C.22)

where the first inequality holds as
a^{2}+b^{2}\geq\frac{a^{2}}{2}-b^{2}
for all
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
, and the second inequality is derived by utilizing the upper and lower bounds in (
C
). We let
\kappa=\min\big\{1,\frac{17}{10\pi^{2}\alpha\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}\big\}
, and then can calculate that

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\big\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}\bigg|^{2}+\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{1}}\bigg|^{2}+\kappa\bigg|\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})}{\partial C_{2}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{17\sigma^{4}}{\pi^{2}d^{2}}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\big[\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}-\kappa\frac{5\alpha\sigma^{6}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{d^{2}}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{17\sigma^{4}}{2\pi^{2}d^{2}}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\kappa}{2}\big[\psi^{\prime}(C_{2})\big]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{5\pi d}\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2d}G_{2}(C_{2})\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\kappa}{1200}\big(\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})\big)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{d}\min\bigg\{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{5\pi d},\frac{\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{600d},\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{375\pi^{2}}\bigg\}\bigg(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2d}G_{2}(C_{2})\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{2\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big),

where
\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}=\min\big\{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{5\pi d},\frac{\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{600d},\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{375\pi^{2}}\big\}
is a constant solely depending on
\alpha
and
\sigma
. Here, the first inequality holds as
\kappa\leq 1
. The second inequality is derived by applying the results of (
C.21
) and (
C
). The third inequality holds as
\kappa\frac{5\alpha\sigma^{6}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{d^{2}}\leq\frac{17\sigma^{4}}{2\pi^{2}d^{2}}
by definition of
\kappa
. The forth inequality is established by substituting the upper bound for
G_{2}(C_{2})
and applying the result of (
C.20
). Finally, we obtain the last inequality by applying the result of (
C.13
). This completes the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove the last conclusion of Theorem
4.5
, i.e. the linear convergence rate for both training loss and parameters, which are presented in the following Theorem
C.9
.

Theorem C.9

(Third conclusion in Theorem
4.5
)
.

Under the same conditions as Theorem
4.5
, the iterates
C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)}
of gradient descent updates with respect to the training loss
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
given in Lemma
C.2
, converge linearly to the unique local minimizer
\mathbf{C}^{*}=[C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*}]^{\top}
of the training loss
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})
. In particular, for all
t\geq 0
, the training loss decays as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\;\leq\;\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big).

Moreover, the coefficient vector
\mathbf{C}^{(t)}=\big[C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)}\big]^{\top}
converges linearly to
\mathbf{C}^{*}
as

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{C}^{(t)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}\;\leq\;\mu_{2,\alpha,\sigma}\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t/2}\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\|_{2}.

Here,
\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}
and
\mu_{2,\alpha,\sigma}
are both positive constants solely depending on
\alpha
and
\sigma
.

Proof of Theorem
C.9
.

Notice that we have demonstrated in the proof of Lemma
C.7
that

\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{16\alpha^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{\sigma^{2}}+9\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\sigma^{4}+4\sigma^{4}}{d}=\iota
for all
[C_{1},C_{2}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{R}
, which can implies that
\|\nabla^{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{2}\iota
. Then we can obtain that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t+1)},C_{2}^{(t+1)})\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\eta\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})\big\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\sqrt{2}\eta^{2}\iota}{2}\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\eta\bigg(1-\frac{\sqrt{2}\eta\iota}{2}\bigg)\big\|\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big),

where the first inequality is established by second-order Taylor’s expansion and the fact that
[C_{1}^{(t+1)},C_{2}^{(t+1)}]^{\top}-[C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)}]^{\top}=\eta\nabla\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})
. The last inequality is obtained by PL condition established in Lemma
C.8
, and
\sqrt{2}\eta\iota\leq 1
. Then by minus
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
on both sides of the inequality above, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t+1)},C_{2}^{(t+1)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\ldots

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t+1}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big).

(C.23)

This completes the proof for the linear convergence rate of loss decay. In the next, we prove the parameter convergence. Notice that for any
[C_{1},C_{2}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{R}
, utilizing the definitions of
G_{*}(C_{2})
and
\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})
in Lemma
C.8
, we have

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}-C_{1}^{*}\big|=\big|\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})+G_{*}(C_{2})-G_{*}(C_{2}^{*})\big|\leq\big|\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})\big|+2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|

where the equality holds by the definition of
\Delta(C_{1},C_{2})
, and the fact that
G_{1}^{*}=G_{*}(C_{2}^{*})
. The second inequality is derived by triangle inequality and
|G_{*}^{\prime}(C_{2})|\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
. Therefore, by
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2a^{2}+2b^{2}
, and the inequality established above, we can further obtain that

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}-C_{1}^{*}\big|^{2}+\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}\leq 2\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\big(1+4\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}}\big)\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}.

(C.24)

On the other hand, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})}{2d}+\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2\pi d}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{2\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{65d}\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2},

(C.25)

where the second inequality holds as
G_{2}(C_{2})\geq\frac{1}{\pi}
as demonstrated in (
C
), and
\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})\geq\frac{4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{65d}
as demonstrated in (
C.17
). Compare (
C.24
) and (
C
), we can obtain that for all
[C_{1},C_{2}]^{\top}\in\mathcal{R}
, it holds

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}-C_{1}^{*}\big|^{2}+\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{130\pi d\max\{2,1+4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}{\min\{65\sigma^{2},4\pi\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big).

(C.26)

On the other hand, for the initialization
C_{1}^{(0)}=C_{2}^{(0)}=0
, we have

\big|G_{*}(C_{2}^{(0)})-G_{*}(C_{2}^{*})\big|\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)
and

\big|\Delta(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})+\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\big|\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{1}{d}\big)
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}^{(0)}-C_{1}^{*}\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\Delta(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})+G_{*}(C_{2}^{(0)})-G_{*}(C_{2}^{*})\big|\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\big|\Delta(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})\big|.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\big\|\mathbf{C}^{(0)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\big|C_{1}^{(0)}-C_{1}^{*}\big|^{2}+\big|C_{2}^{(0)}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\Delta^{2}(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})+\big|C_{2}^{(0)}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}.

(C.27)

Similar to the calculations in (
C
), we can obtain an upper bound for
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})
as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1},C_{2})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})=

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})G_{2}(C_{2})}{2d}+\psi(C_{2})-\psi(C_{2}^{*})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{5\sigma^{2}}{2\pi d}\Delta^{2}(C_{1},C_{2})+\frac{3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}}{2d}\big|C_{2}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2},

(C.28)

where the second inequality holds as
G_{2}(C_{2})\leq\frac{5}{\pi}
as demonstrated in (
C
), and
\psi^{\prime\prime}(C_{2})\geq 3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}
as demonstrated in (
C.17
). Compare (
C.27
) and (
C
), we can conclude that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})-\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\leq\frac{\max\{2\sigma^{2},3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}{d}\big\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}.

(C.29)

For now, we have finished all the required inequalities, and we can finally derive that

\displaystyle\big\|\mathbf{C}^{(t)}-\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}=

\displaystyle\big|C_{1}^{(t)}-C_{1}^{*}\big|^{2}+\big|C_{2}^{(t)}-C_{2}^{*}\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{130\pi d\max\{2,1+4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}{\min\{65\sigma^{2},4\pi\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(t)},C_{2}^{(t)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{130\pi d\max\{2,1+4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}{\min\{65\sigma^{2},4\pi\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t}\Big(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{(0)},C_{2}^{(0)})-\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{train}}(C_{1}^{*},C_{2}^{*})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{130\pi\max\{2,1+4\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}\cdot\max\{2\sigma^{2},3\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}{\min\{65\sigma^{2},4\pi\alpha^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}}\}}\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t}\big\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{2,\alpha,\sigma}^{2}\bigg(1-\frac{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}{d}\bigg)^{t}\big\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\big\|_{2}^{2}.

Here, the inequality is established by (
C.26
). The second inequality is derived by (
C
). And the last inequality is obtained by implying the results of (
C.29
).
This completes the proof.

∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4.7

In this section, we present the proof of Theorem
4.7
. We begin by outlining the key insights underlying the argument. Corollary
4.3
establishes that the weight prediction produced by an
L
-layer transformer, when parameterized as in Theorem
4.1
, converges to the maximum-margin solution on the context data. Moreover, Theorem
4.5
shows that, under supervision from a one-step GD, the parameter matrices

\mathbf{V}^{(t)}
and
\mathbf{W}^{(t)}
of the self-attention layer converge to the specific parameterization described in Theorem
4.1
