. Taken together, these results imply that it suffices to derive an upper bound on the Euclidean distance between the maximum-margin solution

\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})
and the ground truth
\bm{\theta}^{*}
. We first introduce several lemmas which will be utilized in proof.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{x}_{i}
are i.i.d. samples from a
d
-dimensional log-concave distribution with a positive definite covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
for all
i\in[n]
. Then for any
\delta>0
and any
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, the following conclusion holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle|\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle|\geq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{2n},\quad\text{for all }i\in[n].

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

For all
i\in[n]
, we know that
\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle
follows a one-dimensional log-concave distribution with mean
\mu_{l}=\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{i}]\rangle
and variance
\sigma_{l}^{2}=\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{\theta}
. This implies that
\frac{\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle-\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}
follows one-dimensional isotropic log-concave distribution. Consequently, for any
s>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle|<s\big)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle-\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}+\frac{\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}\bigg|<\frac{s}{\sigma_{l}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(-\frac{s}{\sigma_{l}}-\frac{\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}\leq\frac{\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle-\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}<\frac{s}{\sigma_{l}}-\frac{\mu_{l}}{\sigma_{l}}\bigg)\leq\frac{2s}{\sigma_{l}},

where the last inequality holds as the probability density function of isotropic log concave distribution is always smaller than

1
as demonstrated in Lemma
F.23
.
Taking a union bound for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\min_{i\in[n]}|\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle|<s\bigg)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\big(|\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle|<s\big)\leq\frac{2ns}{\sigma_{l}}.

Let the right hand side of the above inequality smaller than
\delta
, we derive that
s\geq\frac{\sigma_{l}\delta}{2n}\geq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{2n}
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{x}_{i}
are i.i.d. samples from a
d
-dimensional log-concave distribution with mean vector
\bm{\mu}
and a positive definite covariance matrix
\bm{\Sigma}
for all
i\in[n]
. Then for any
\delta>0
, the following conclusion holds with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\sqrt{d}+\log\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)\bigg),\quad\text{for all }i\in[n].

Proof.

Notice that
\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}=\bm{\Sigma}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-\bm{\mu})
follows a
d
-dimensional isotropic log-concave distribution. Then by Lemma
F.25
, for any
s>1
and
i\in[n]
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}\|_{2}\geq s\sqrt{d}\big)\leq e^{-\frac{s\sqrt{d}}{c}},

where
c
is an absolute positive constant. In addition, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\leq\Big\|\bm{\Sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}\Big\|_{2}+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})}\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}\|_{2}+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}.

Combine these two results, we can obtain that for any
s>1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})}s\sqrt{d}+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\|\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{i}\|_{2}\geq s\sqrt{d}\big)\leq e^{-\frac{s\sqrt{d}}{c}}.

Taking a union bound for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\max_{i\in[n]}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})}s\sqrt{d}+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\bigg)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\big(\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\bm{\Sigma})}s\sqrt{d}+\|\bm{\mu}\|_{2}\big)\leq ne^{-\frac{s\sqrt{d}}{c}}.

Let the right hand side of the above inequality smaller than
\delta
, we derive that
s\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{d}}\log\big(\frac{n}{\delta}\big)
. Therefore, by setting
s=1+\frac{c}{\sqrt{d}}\log\big(\frac{n}{\delta}\big)
, we complete the proof.
∎

Now, we are ready to prove Theorem
4.7

Proof of Theorem
4.7
.

Combining the results of Theorem
B.1
, and Theorem
4.5
, we know that the weight prediction
\bm{\theta}_{L}=[\mathtt{TF}(\mathbf{Z}_{0};[\mathbf{V}^{(t)}]^{\otimes L},[\mathbf{W}^{(t)}]^{\otimes L})]_{d+1:2d,n+1}
of the looped transformers equals to that obtained by applying normalized gradient descent on rescaled dataset
\{(C_{2}^{(t)}\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
with a learning rate
C_{1}^{(t)}/C_{2}^{(t)}
. In addition, the rescaled dataset
\{(C_{2}^{(t)}\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
shares the same maximum margin solution with the original context data, i.e.
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})
. When
t\geq\Omega\big(\frac{d[\log(3\|\mathbf{C}^{*}\|_{2})-\log(1\wedge\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2})]}{\eta\mu_{1,\alpha,\sigma}}\big)
, the convergence results in Theorem
4.5
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{2}\leq C_{1}^{(t)}\leq 2\alpha e^{\sigma^{2}/2};\quad\frac{1}{2}\leq C_{2}^{(t)}\leq 2.

Consequently, we have
\frac{C_{1}^{(t)}}{C_{2}^{(t)}}=\Theta(\alpha)\leq\mathcal{O}(1)
, and Theorem
4.3
guarantees that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\bm{\theta}_{L}}{\|\bm{\theta}_{L}\|_{2}}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\bigg\|_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\alpha L}\bigg).

(D.1)

This result demonstrates that it suffices to provide an upper bound for
\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2}
, which can be converted to the test error of
\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})
as

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\bm{\theta}^{*}\big\|_{2}=

\displaystyle 2\sin\bigg(\frac{\angle(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0}),\bm{\theta}^{*})}{2}\bigg)\leq\angle(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0}),\bm{\theta}^{*})

(D.2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{-}^{-1}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{x}}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0}),\mathbf{x}\rangle)\neq\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\big)=c_{-}^{-1}p\big(\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\big),

(D.3)

where the last inequality holds by Lemma
F.24
, and
c_{-}
is an absolute positive constant. In addition,
p(\bm{\theta})
is defined as:

\displaystyle p(\bm{\theta})=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{x}}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\neq\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\big)

for all
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, exactly representing the test error of
\bm{\theta}
. In the following, we focus on providing the upper bound for this term. By Lemma
D.1
and Lemma
D.2
, with probability at least
1-\frac{2}{3}\delta
, it holds that

\displaystyle\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\bm{\theta}^{*},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle\geq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{6n};\ \max_{i\in[n]}\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\leq c_{1}\bigg(\sqrt{d}+\log\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)\bigg),

(D.4)

where
c_{1}
is an absolute positive constant. We set
\epsilon=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{12c_{1}n\big(\sqrt{d}+\log(n\delta^{-1})\big)}
, and denote
N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)
as the
\epsilon
-net on the
d
-dimensional unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
Then by the definition of
\epsilon
-net, there exist
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}\in N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)
such that
\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
. Then for
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}
, it can still achieve zero classification error on context dataset
\{(\mathbf{x}_{i},y_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}
, as for any
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}\rangle\geq

\displaystyle\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\rangle+y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\rangle

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\bm{\theta}^{*}\rangle-\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{6n}-c_{1}\bigg(\sqrt{d}+\log\Big(\frac{n}{\delta}\Big)\bigg)\epsilon\geq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{12n}.

Here, the second inequality holds as
\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\rangle\geq\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\bm{\theta}^{*}\rangle
by the definition of SVM solution, and
y_{i}\langle\mathbf{x}_{i},\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\rangle\geq-\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{\theta}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\|_{2}
by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. The third inequality is derived by (
D.4
), and the last inequality holds by our definition of
\epsilon
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(p(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}})\geq\varepsilon\big)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\big\{\exists\ \bm{\theta}\in N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon):p(\bm{\theta})\geq\varepsilon,\ \text{and}\ \min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle>0\big\}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\big|N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)\big|e^{-n\varepsilon}\leq\bigg(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{d}e^{-n\varepsilon}.

Here, the second inequality holds as for any
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
,
\mathbb{P}(\min_{i\in[n]}y_{i}\langle\bm{\theta},\mathbf{x}_{i}\rangle>0)\leq(1-p(\bm{\theta}))^{n}\leq e^{-np(\bm{\theta})}
, and we take an union bound for all
\bm{\theta}\in N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)
. The last inequality holds as Lemma
F.26
guarantees that
\big|N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)\big|\leq\big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\big)^{d}
. By setting
\big(\frac{3}{\epsilon}\big)^{d}e^{-n\varepsilon}\leq\frac{\delta}{3}
and replacing the definition of
\epsilon
, we can derive that
\varepsilon=\Theta\Big(\frac{d\log\big(\frac{n\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta^{2}}\big)}{n}\Big)
. Therefore, combined with fact that (
D.4
) holds with probability at least
1-\frac{2\delta}{3}
, we can conclude that

\displaystyle p(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}})\leq\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{d\big[\log(n\sqrt{d})-\log(\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta^{2})\big]}{n}\bigg)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
.
On the other hand, we can further calculate that

\displaystyle p\big(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\big)\leq

\displaystyle p(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}})+\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{x}}}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0}),\mathbf{x}\rangle)\neq\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\widetilde{\bm{\theta}},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle p(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}})+c_{+}\pi\big\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}\big\|_{2}\leq p(\widetilde{\bm{\theta}})+c_{+}\pi\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{d\big[\log(n\sqrt{d})-\log(\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta^{2})\big]}{n}\bigg).

Here, the second inequality holds by Lemma
F.24
, where
c_{+}
is a positive absolute constant. The third inequality is derived as
\big\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}\big\|_{2}\leq\epsilon
by the choice of
\widetilde{\bm{\theta}}
in
N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)
. And the last inequality holds as
\epsilon=\frac{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})}\delta}{12c_{1}n\big(\sqrt{d}+\log(n\delta^{-1})\big)}\leq\mathcal{O}\big(\frac{d}{n}\big)
. Combined this result with (
D.1
) and (
D.2
), we finally conclude that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\bm{\theta}_{L}}{\|\bm{\theta}_{L}\|_{2}}-\bm{\theta}^{*}\bigg\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\bm{\theta}_{L}}{\|\bm{\theta}_{L}\|_{2}}-\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})\bigg\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{SVM}}(\mathbf{Z}_{0})-\bm{\theta}^{*}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\bigg(\frac{\log n}{\alpha L}+\frac{d\log\big(\max\{n,d,\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Sigma})^{-1},\delta^{-1}\}\big)}{n}\bigg).

This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix E
Additional experimental results

In this section, we further validate the equivalence between softmax transformers and normalized gradient descent in Theorem
4.1
on real-world datasets. We consider two datasets from different modalities: MNIST for image classification and SST-2 for sentiment classification. For MNIST, we use the binary classification task between digits
1
and
2
, and convert each image into a
d=20
dimensional vector representation using a trained CNN encoder. For SST-2, we use a pretrained BERT encoder followed by a projection layer to obtain
d=20
dimensional sentence representations. In both cases, given the encoded feature vector
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and binary label
y_{i}\in\{-1,1\}
, we form the signed feature vector
\mathbf{z}_{i}=y_{i}\cdot\mathbf{x}_{i}
and construct the input matrix
\mathbf{Z}_{0}
in the same form as (
3.1
). We then compare the hidden-layer outputs of the manually constructed looped softmax transformer in Theorem
4.1
with the corresponding normalized-gradient-descent iterates over
L=30
layers/iterations.

Figure 8
:

Difference between the normalized transformer output and the normalized NGD iterate on MNIST and SST-2.

Figure
8
reports the discrepancy

\big\|\frac{\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{TF}}}{\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{TF}}\|_{2}}-\frac{\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{NGD}}}{\|\bm{\theta}_{\mathrm{NGD}}\|_{2}}\big\|_{2}
between the transformer output and the NGD iterate across layers. The discrepancy remains negligible on both MNIST and SST-2 throughout the entire trajectory. This shows that, after raw inputs are converted into vector representations by standard encoders, the constructed softmax transformer continues to closely match the NGD dynamics. These results provide empirical evidence that the equivalence in Theorem
4.1
is robust across different input modalities, including image and text data.

Appendix F
Technical lemmas

F.1
Expectation calculations

Lemma F.1
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]-2na\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}B\Bigg|\leq f_{1}(a,\sigma).

Here,
B=\mathbb{E}[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}\Phi(-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle\sigma)]
is an absolute constant independent of
a,n
satisfying that
\big|B-\frac{1}{2}\big|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{d}
, and
f_{1}(a,\sigma)
is an analytic function of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.1
.

We denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
, and
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
, then we have
K_{1}^{2}+K_{2}^{2}=1
, and the p.d.f. of
K_{1}
is given in Lemma
F.20
.
In the next, by utilizing the Laplace transform identity
\frac{1}{S}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sS}ds
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\!\bigg(-s\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s.

Substituting this into the expectation and utilizing Fubini’s Theorem to exchange the order of integral calculations, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{1}(s/n,1+a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{2}(s/n,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg],

(F.1)

where the second equation holds by the symmetries among
x_{1},\ldots,x_{n}
and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}
, and the third equation holds by replacing
s^{\prime}
with
s/n
in the integral.
In addition, the terms
A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
are defined as:

\displaystyle A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle M(\lambda,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big].

For the term
A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
, by the fact that
1-z\leq e^{-z}\leq 1
for all
z>0
, we have that

\displaystyle A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\geq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big]-\lambda\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-(a+\alpha)K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-(a+\alpha)K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\leq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big].

(F.2)

We can establish the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
based on the inequalities (
F.1
) above. We first derive the lower bound as

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+2a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+2a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{2}^{2}(1+2a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1}{n}e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(2a+1)\sigma)\int_{0}^{\infty}s\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{2}^{2}(1+2a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1}{n}e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(2a+1)\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}se^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{K_{2}^{2}(1+2a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1}{n}e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(2a+1)\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}-\frac{c_{1}(a\sigma)}{n}\geq\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-(a+1)\sigma)-1

(F.3)

The first equality is true because for a normal variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[z^{2}e^{-cz}]=\sigma^{2}(c^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}
and
\mathbb{E}[e^{-c|z|}]=2e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the third follows from direct calculation. The penultimate inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19
, where
c_{1}(a)
is a constant solely depending on
a
. Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\big(K_{2}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}-\frac{c_{1}(a\sigma)}{n}\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}((1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)}{\Phi(-a\sigma)}e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}+1.

(F.4)

To calculate the upper and lower bounds for the term
(II)
is a little tricky, and we first calculate the derivatives of
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
w.r.t.
\lambda
as

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Big|-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-(\alpha+a)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}x_{1}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|x_{1}|e^{-(\alpha+a)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}\big]\leq 2\sigma e^{(a+\alpha)^{2}\sigma^{2}},

which implies that both
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
is Lipschitz continuous w.r.t.
\lambda
. Therefore, we can further derive that

\displaystyle|A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1}))A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})+A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})(A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})|+|A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})|(|A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|+|A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|)

\displaystyle+|A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|\leq 8\lambda\sigma^{3}ae^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}+4\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}

where the last inequality holds by using the Lipschitz continuous properties of
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
, and the facts that
A_{2}(0,1,a,K_{1})=-2\sigma^{2}K_{2}e^{\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma),A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})=-2a\sigma^{2}K_{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)
and
|K_{1}|,|K_{2}|\leq 1
. With the inequality established above and the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})\leq 4a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{(a^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)\Phi(\!-a\!K_{1}\sigma)+8\lambda\sigma^{3}ae^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}+4\lambda^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}};

\displaystyle A_{2}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})\geq 4a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{(a^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)\Phi(\!-a\!K_{1}\sigma)-8\lambda\sigma^{3}ae^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}-4\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}.

(F.5)

Now, we are ready to derive the lower and upper bounds for the term
(II)
based on (
F.1
). We first derive the lower bound as

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{2}(s/n,1,a,K_{1})A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 4a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{(a^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)\Phi(\!-a\!K_{1}\sigma)\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{8\sigma^{3}ae^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s-\frac{4\sigma^{2}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{(a^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)\Phi(\!-a\!K_{1}\sigma)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{8\sigma^{3}ae^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\bigg]-\frac{8\sigma^{2}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)-\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n},

(F.6)

where
c_{3}(a,\sigma)=\frac{2a\sigma^{3}e^{4(a\vee 1)^{2}4\sigma^{2}}}{\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma)}
, a continuous function of
a
and
\sigma
. Here the last equation is derived by Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of the integral, and the last inequality is established by Lemma
F.19
. Similarly, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle(II)\leq

\displaystyle 4a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{(a^{2}+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)\Phi(\!-a\!K_{1}\sigma)\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{8a\sigma^{3}e^{5(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{4\sigma^{2}e^{8(a\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2a\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)+\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.7)

Substituting the results of (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), and (
F.1
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.2
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d standard Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]-a^{2}\sigma^{4}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{d}\bigg)\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{2}(a,\sigma)}{n},

where
f_{2}(a,\sigma)
is an analytic function of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.2
.

The proof of this lemma is quite similar to that of Lemma
F.1
. We repeatedly use the previous notations that
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
, and
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. For this lemma, we leverage another identity
\frac{1}{S^{2}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}se^{-sS}ds
to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}s\exp\!\bigg(\!-\!s\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we substitute the identity above into the expectation and utilize Fubini’s Theorem to exchange the order of integral calculations to obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}sA_{1}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s[A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg].

(F.8)

Here,
A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
. We can use similar procedures in (
F.1
) and (
F.1
) from the proof of Lemma
F.1
to calculate the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}K_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(\!-\!2a\sigma K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}se^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1}{n}e^{\frac{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}2\Phi(\!-\!3a\sigma K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\mathrm{d}s\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1\big)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(\!-\!2a\sigma K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1}{n}e^{\frac{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}4\Phi(\!-\!3a\sigma K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2[\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)]^{2}}-\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}\geq\frac{\sigma^{2}e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(\!-\!2a\sigma)}{2}-1

(F.9)

The first equality is true because for a normal random variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[z^{2}e^{-cz}]=\sigma^{2}(c^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}
and
\mathbb{E}[e^{-c|z|}]=2e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the third follows from direct calculation. The penultimate inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19
, where
c_{1}(a)
is a constant solely depending on
a
. Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1\big)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(\!-\!2a\sigma K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1\big)e^{\frac{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2a\sigma K_{1})}{2[\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)]^{2}}+\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)}{2\Phi^{2}(a\sigma)}e^{\frac{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}+1,

(F.10)

With the Lipschitz continuity of
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
derived in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we can also demonstrate that

\displaystyle|[A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}-[A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})||A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})+A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})||A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|+|A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\lambda a\sigma^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}+4\lambda^{2}\sigma^{2}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}.

Now, by using the triangle inequality to establish upper and lower bounds for
[A_{2}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}
from the inequalities above, we are ready to derive the lower and upper bounds for the term
(II)
. We first derive the lower bound as

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s[A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 4a^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}K_{2}^{2}[\Phi(\!-a\sigma K_{1})]^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s-\frac{8a\sigma^{3}e^{9a^{2}/2}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{4\sigma^{2}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{3}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4a^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}K_{2}^{2}[\Phi(\!-a\sigma K_{1})]^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{16a\sigma^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\bigg]-\frac{24e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{4}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{4}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle a^{2}\sigma^{4}K_{2}^{2}-\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n},

(F.11)

where
c_{3}(a,\sigma)=\frac{3e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{3}}}{\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma)}
is a positive continuous function of
a
and
\sigma
. Here the last equation is derived by Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of the integral, and the last inequality is established by Lemma
F.19
. Similarly, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle(II)\leq

\displaystyle 4a^{2}\sigma^{4}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}K_{2}^{2}[\Phi(\!-a\sigma K_{1})]^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{8a\sigma^{3}e^{9a^{2},\sigma^{2}/2}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{4\sigma^{2}e^{8a^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{3}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a^{2}\sigma^{4}K_{2}^{2}+\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.12)

Substituting the results of (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), and (
F.1
) into (
F.1
), and utilizing the fact that
\mathbb{E}[K_{2}^{2}]=1-\frac{1}{d}
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.3
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]-nB_{a,\sigma,1}\Bigg|\leq f_{3}(a,\sigma)+\frac{nf_{4}(a,\sigma)}{d},

where
B_{a,\sigma,1}=\frac{2\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{\pi}
, and
f_{3}(a,\sigma),f_{4}(a,\sigma)
are both smooth functions of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.3
.

We repeatedly use the previous notations that
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
, and leverage
\frac{1}{S}=\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-sS}ds
to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\exp\!\bigg(\!-\!s\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\bigg)\mathrm{d}s.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we substitute the identity above into the expectation and utilize Fubini’s Theorem to exchange the order of integral calculations to obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{3}(s/n,1+a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{4}(s/n,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg],

(F.13)

Here,
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
, and the terms
A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
are defined as:

\displaystyle A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big].

We use the fact
1-z\leq e^{-z}\leq 1
to derive the upper and lower bounds for
A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
as

\displaystyle A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\geq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big]-\lambda\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(\alpha+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(\alpha+a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\leq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big].

We can establish the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
based on the inequalities above as,

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+2a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+2a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\bigg(2\big(1+K_{1}^{2}(1+a)^{2}\sigma^{2}\big)e^{\frac{K_{1}^{2}(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}K_{1}(1+a)\sigma\bigg)

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{K_{2}^{2}(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma^{2}\big(2\big(1+K_{1}^{2}(1+2a)^{2}\sigma^{2}\big)e^{\frac{K_{1}^{2}(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(2a+1)\sigma)-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}K_{1}(1+2a)\sigma\big)}{n}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{\frac{K_{2}^{2}(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\sigma(a+1)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma^{2}(8a^{2}+10a+5)}{n}e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{\sigma(a+1)}{\Phi(-a\sigma)}e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}-1

(F.14)

The first equality is true because for a standard normal variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[|z|^{2}e^{-c|z|}]=\sigma^{2}\big(2(1+c^{2}\sigma^{2})e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)-c\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\big)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the fact that
|K_{1}|,|K_{2}|\leq 1
. The last inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19
, where
c_{1}(a)
is a constant solely depending on
a
. Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}(2a^{2}+5a+5)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}(2a^{2}+5a+5)}{\Phi(-a\sigma)}e^{\frac{(2a+1)\sigma^{2}}{2}}+1

(F.15)

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we first calculate the derivative of
A_{4}(\lambda,a,K_{1})
w.r.t.
\lambda
as

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Big|-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-(a+\alpha)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|\widetilde{x}_{1}|e^{-(a+\alpha)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}|K_{1}\big]\leq\sigma(2|a+\alpha|\sigma+1)e^{(a+\alpha)^{2}\sigma^{2}},

which implies that both
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and is Lipschitz continuous w.r.t.
\lambda
. Therefore, we can further derive that

\displaystyle|A_{4}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(A_{4}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1}))A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})+A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})(A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{4}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})|+|A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|A_{4}(\lambda,1,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})|(|A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1}))|+|A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|)

\displaystyle+|A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})||A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 30\lambda\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{9(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}+25\lambda^{2}\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}},

where the last inequality holds by using the Lipschitz continuous properties of
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
, the facts that
A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma e^{K_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}-2\sigma^{2}K_{1}\Phi(-K_{1}\sigma)e^{\sigma^{2}/2}
,
A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})=\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\sigma e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}/2}-2aK_{1}\sigma^{2}\Phi(-aK_{1}\sigma)e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}
and
|K_{1}|,|K_{2}|\leq 1
. With the inequality established above and the triangle inequality,
we calculate the lower and upper bounds for the term
(II)
as

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{4}(s/n,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s-

\displaystyle-\frac{30\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{5(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\!\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{25\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8(|a|\vee 1)^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{30\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{5(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\!\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{50\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8(|a|\vee 1)^{2}}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{2e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-aK_{1}\sigma)}-\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n},

(F.16)

where
c_{3}(a,\sigma)=\frac{8\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{7(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma)}
, a positive continuous function of
a
and
\sigma
. Here the last equation is derived by Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of the integral, and the last inequality is established by Lemma
F.19
. Similarly, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle(II)\leq

\displaystyle A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{30\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{5(|a|\vee 1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{25\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8(|a|\vee 1)^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{2e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-aK_{1}\sigma)}+\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.17)

To further derive a concrete bounds for the leading term
F(a,K_{1})=\frac{A_{4}(0,1,a,K_{1})A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{2e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-aK_{1})}
, we consider the second order Taylor’s expansion of
F(a,K_{1})
at
K_{1}=0
. By utilizing the conclusion that
K_{1}
has a symmetric distribution, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(a,K_{1})]=F(a,0)+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(a,\xi)K_{1}^{2}}{2}\bigg],

where
\xi
is a random variable between
0
and
K_{1}
. Since the function
F(a,k)
is analytic w.r.t.
k
on
[-1,1]
, and
|K_{1}|\leq 1
(hence
|\xi|\leq 1
), its second-order derivative
F^{\prime\prime}(a,k)
is continuous and bounded on this compact interval. Therefore, there exists a constant
c_{4}(a,\sigma)
such that
|F^{\prime\prime}(a,\xi)|\leq c_{4}(a,\sigma)
. By
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}^{2}]=1/d
and
F(a,0)=\frac{2\sigma^{2}e^{\sigma^{2}/2}}{\pi}
, we can further derive that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}[F(a,K_{1})]-\frac{2\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\bigg|\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{c_{4}(a,\sigma)}{d}\bigg].

(F.18)

Substituting these results of (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), and (
F.18
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.4
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d standard Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]-\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\pi}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{5}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{6}(a,\sigma)}{d},

where

f_{5}(a,\sigma)
and
f_{6}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.4
.

We repeatedly use the previous notations that
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
, and
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
.
Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.2
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}sA_{3}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s[A_{4}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg].

(F.19)

Here,
A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.3
. Through similar calculation procedures in (
F.1
) and (
F.1
) from the proof of Lemma
F.3
, we can calculate the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\bigg(2\big(1+4K_{1}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}\big)e^{2K_{1}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(\!-\!2K_{1}a\sigma)-2\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}K_{1}a\sigma\bigg)e^{2K_{2}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{2\big(1+9K_{1}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}\big)e^{\frac{9K_{1}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(\!-\!3K_{1}a\sigma)-\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}3K_{1}a\sigma}{n}\sigma^{2}e^{\frac{9K_{2}^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-2a\sigma e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{2\sigma^{2}(18a^{2}+3a+2)}{n}e^{\frac{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\frac{a\sigma}{2\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma)}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}-1.

(F.20)

The first equality is true because for a normal random variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[|z|^{2}e^{-c|z|}]=\sigma^{2}\big(2(1+c^{2}\sigma^{2})e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)-c\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2}{\pi}}\big)
and
\mathbb{E}[e^{-c|z|}]=2e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the third follows from direct calculation. The penultimate inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19

Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sigma^{2}\big(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}+1\big)e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(4a^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}}{2\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma)}+1.

(F.21)

With the Lipschitz continuity of
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
derived in the proof of Lemma
F.3
, we can also demonstrate that

\displaystyle|[A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}-[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})||A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})+A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})||A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|+|A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})-A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 10\lambda\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}+25\lambda^{2}\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}},

Now, by using the triangle inequality to establish upper and lower bounds for
[A_{4}(\lambda,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}
from the inequalities above, we are ready to derive the lower and upper bounds for the term
(II)
. We first derive the lower bound as

\displaystyle(II)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s[A_{4}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s-\frac{10\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{25\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{3}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{20\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\frac{150\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{4}}{\big(\sum_{i=3}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{4}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}}{4e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}-\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n},

(F.22)

where
c_{3}(a,\sigma)=\frac{2\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{3a^{2}}\sigma^{2}}{\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma)}
, a positive continuous function of
a
and
\sigma
. Here the last equation is derived by Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of the integral, and the last inequality is established by Lemma
F.19
. Similarly, we can also obtain that

\displaystyle(II)\leq

\displaystyle[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s+\frac{10\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{3}e^{9a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle+\frac{25\max\{{a,1,\sigma}\}^{4}e^{8a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{3}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}}{4e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}+\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.23)

To further derive a concrete bounds for the leading term
F(a,K_{1})=\frac{[A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})]^{2}}{4e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}
, we consider the forth order Taylor’s expansion of
F(a,K_{1})
at
K_{1}=0
. By utilizing the conclusion that
K_{1}
has a symmetric distribution, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[F(a,K_{1})]=F(a,0)+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(a,\xi)K_{1}^{2}}{2}\bigg],

where
\xi
is a random variable between
0
and
K_{1}
. Since the function
F(a,k)
is analytic w.r.t.
k
on
[-1,1]
, and
|K_{1}|\leq 1
(hence
|\xi|\leq 1
), its fourth derivative
F^{(2)}(a,k)
is continuous and bounded on this compact interval. Therefore, there exists a constant
c_{4}(a,\sigma)
such that
|F^{(4)}(a,\xi)|\leq c_{4}(a,\sigma)
. By the fact that
F(a,0)=\frac{2}{\pi}\sigma^{2}
and
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}^{2}]=1/d
,
we finally derive that

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}[F(a,K_{1})]-\frac{2\sigma^{2}}{\pi}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{4}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

(F.24)

Substituting these results of (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), (
F.1
), and (
F.24
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.5
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i}+ax_{i})}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]-B_{a,2}e^{(a+\frac{1}{2})\sigma^{2}}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{7}(a,\sigma)}{n},

where
B_{a,2}=\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}\big]
. It satisfies that
\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}\big]-1\big|\leq\frac{f_{8}(a,\sigma)}{d}
, where
f_{7}(a,\sigma),f_{8}(a,\sigma)
are both continuous functions of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.5
.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.1
to obtain that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{i}}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{i}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{5}(s/n,1+a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg],

(F.25)

Here,
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
, and the terms
A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
is defined as:

\displaystyle A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big].

We use the fact
1-z\leq e^{-z}\leq 1
to derive the upper and lower bounds for
A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
as

\displaystyle A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\geq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|K_{1}\big]-\lambda\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(\alpha+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(\alpha+a)x_{1}}|K_{1}\big];

\displaystyle A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})\leq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}|K_{1}\big].

We can establish the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
based on the inequalities above as,

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+2a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+2a)x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(2a+1)\sigma)}{n}e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!(a+1)\sigma)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]-\frac{2e^{\frac{(2a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}e^{\frac{2a+1}{2}\sigma^{2}}-\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.26)

The first equality is true because for a normal random variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[e^{-c|z|}]=2e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the fact that
|K_{1}|,|K_{2}|\leq 1
. The last inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19
. Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)e^{\frac{(a+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]\leq\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}e^{\frac{2a+1}{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.27)

In addition, by utilizing the Taylor’s expansion regarding the function
\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}\big]
w.r.t.
K_{1}
at
K_{1}=0
, we can derive that
\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}(a+1)\sigma)}{\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}a\sigma)}\big]-1\big|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{d}
.
∎

Lemma F.6
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]-\frac{B_{a,3}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{9}(a,\sigma)}{n^{2}}.

where
B_{a,3}=\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}\big]
.
It satisfies that
\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}\big]-1\big|\leq\frac{f_{10}(a,\sigma)}{d}
, where
f_{9}(a,\sigma),f_{10}(a,\sigma)
are both continuous functions of
a
and
\sigma
, and irrelevant with
n,d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.6
.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.2
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2aK_{2}x_{i}}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]=\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2aK_{2}x_{i}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}sA_{5}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg].

(F.28)

Here,
A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.5
. Through similar calculation procedures in
the proof of Lemma
F.5
, we can calculate the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s-\frac{2\Phi(\!-\!3aK_{1}\sigma)}{n}e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}\big(e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]-\frac{4e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{3}}{\big(\sum_{i=2}^{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{3}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}-\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.29)

The first equality is true because for a normal random variable
z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
and any scalar
c
, we have
\mathbb{E}[e^{-c|z|}]=2e^{c^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-c\sigma)
. The second equality is obtained by applying Fubini’s theorem to exchange the order of integration, and the fact that
|K_{1}|,|K_{2}|\leq 1
. The last inequality is derived by applying Lemma
F.19
. Following a similar (but simpler) calculation, we can also get the upper bound for
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}|K_{1}\big][M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{n^{2}}{\sum_{i=2}^{n}\big(e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+K_{2}x_{i})}\big)^{2}}\Big|K_{1}\bigg]\leq\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}+\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.30)

In addition, by utilizing the Taylor’s expansion regarding the function
\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}\big]
w.r.t.
K_{1}
at
K_{1}=0
, we can derive that
\big|\mathbb{E}\big[\frac{\Phi(\!-\!2aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{2}(\!-\!aK_{1}\sigma)}\big]-1\big|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{d}
.
∎

Lemma F.7
.

Let

x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-a\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]-nB_{a,4}\Bigg|\leq f_{11}(a,\sigma).

Here,
B_{a,4}=\mathbb{E}[-2\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}K_{2}^{2}(a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}+1)\Phi(\!-\!K_{1}\sigma)]
, satisfying that
|B_{a,4}+\sigma^{4}e^{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2}}(a^{2}\sigma^{2}+1)|\leq\frac{f_{12}(a,\sigma)}{d}
, and
f_{11}(a,\sigma)
,
f_{12}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
while irrelevant with
n
and
d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.7
.

We repeatedly use the previous notations that
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
, and
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
.
Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\frac{\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-K_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+ax_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}^{2}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{3}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(1+a)|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-K_{2}(1+a)x_{1}}x_{1}^{3}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-K_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+a|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-K_{2}(x_{1}+ax_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{6}(s/n,1+a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}A_{2}(s/n,1,a,K_{1})A_{1}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg].

(F.31)

Here,
A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
. In addition, the term
A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
is defined as:

\displaystyle A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})=\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\alpha K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-\alpha K_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{3}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big].

And we can further calculate the derivative of
A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
w.r.t.
\lambda
as

\displaystyle\bigg|\frac{\mathrm{d}A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})}{\mathrm{d}\lambda}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\Big|-\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-(\alpha+a)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}x_{1}^{3}e^{-\lambda e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[|x_{1}|^{3}e^{-(\alpha+a)(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}\big]\leq 2\sqrt{15}\sigma^{3}e^{(a+\alpha)^{2}\sigma^{2}},

which implies that
A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
is Lipschitz continuous w.r.t.
\lambda
, and
|A_{6}(s/n,1+a,a,K_{1})-A_{6}(0,1+a,a,K_{1})|\leq 2\sqrt{15}\sigma^{3}e^{(a+\alpha)^{2}}s/n
. Consequently, we can establish the upper and lower bounds for the term
(I)
as

\displaystyle(I)\geq
