Lemma F.15
.

Let

x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2aK_{2}x_{i}}x_{i}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]+\frac{2a\sigma^{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{27}(a,\sigma)}{n^{2}}+\frac{f_{28}(a,\sigma)}{nd}.

Here,
f_{27}(a,\sigma)
and
f_{28}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
while irrelevant with
n
and
d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.15
.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.9
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2aK_{2}x_{i}}x_{i}}{\big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\big)^{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-2aK_{2}x_{i}}x_{i}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{\prime}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}x_{1}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-2aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-2aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}x_{1}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}sA_{2}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg].

(F.68)

Here,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
.
By using the Lipschitz continuity of
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
established in the proof of Lemma
F.1
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\bigg|(I)+\frac{A_{2}(0,2a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(I)+\frac{a\sigma^{2}K_{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-2aK_{1}\sigma)}{\Phi^{2}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.69)

Specifically, by considering Taylor’s expansion of the function above at
K_{1}=0
, we can further derive that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[K_{2}(I)]+2a\sigma^{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\big|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

(F.70)

Substituting the results of (
F.69
), and (
F.70
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.16
.

Let

x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}x_{i_{3}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]+a^{3}\sigma^{6}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{29}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{30}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

Here,
f_{29}(a,\sigma)
,
f_{30}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
while irrelevant with
n
and
d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.16
.

Following the identity that
\frac{1}{S^{n}}=\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{n-1}e^{-sS}\mathrm{d}s
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}x_{i_{3}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}x_{i_{1}}x_{i_{2}}x_{i_{3}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{3}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{3n(n-1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n(n-1)(n-2)}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+|\widetilde{x}_{3}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})}x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{3}|+K_{2}x_{3})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}^{3}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{3(n-1)}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}x_{1}x_{2}^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+|\widetilde{x}_{3}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})}x_{1}x_{2}x_{3}

\displaystyle\cdot e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{3}|+K_{2}x_{3})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{6}(s/n,3a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{3(n-1)}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})A_{1}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{2}^{3}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s}_{(III)}\bigg].

(F.71)

Here,
A_{1}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
and
A_{6}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
shares the same definition as in the proof in Lemma
F.7
. Through similar procedures in the proof of Lemma
F.10
(by utilizing the Lipschitz continuities of these functions), we can derive that:

\displaystyle\bigg|(I)+\frac{A_{6}(0,3a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(I)+\frac{3a\sigma^{4}K_{2}e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\big(3+9a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{2}^{2}\big)\Phi(-3aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n};

\displaystyle\bigg|(II)+\frac{A_{1}(0,2a,a,K_{1})A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(II)+\frac{a\sigma^{4}K_{2}(1+4a^{2}K_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2})e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-2aK_{1}\sigma)}{\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.72)

Specifically, for the term
(IV)
, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|(III)+\frac{A_{2}^{3}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(IV)+2a^{3}\sigma^{6}K_{2}^{3}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.73)

Lastly, by utilizing Taylor’s expansion regarding the function
\mathbb{E}\big[2a^{3}\sigma^{6}K_{2}^{4}\big]
w.r.t.
K_{1}
at
K_{1}=0
, and the facts that
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}^{2}]=1/d
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[K_{2}(III)\big]+2a^{3}\sigma^{6}\Big|\leq\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{c_{4}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

(F.74)

Substituting the results of (
F.1
), (
F.73
), and (
F.74
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.17
.

Let

x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|x_{i_{3}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]+\frac{2}{\pi}a\sigma^{4}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{31}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{f_{32}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

Here,
f_{30}(a,\sigma)
,
f_{31}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
while irrelevant with
n
and
d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.17
.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.16
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{3}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|x_{i_{3}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\!(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\!\!\sum_{i_{1},i_{2},i_{3}=1}^{n}\!\!e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{3}}|)-aK_{2}(x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}}+x_{i_{3}})}|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}||\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|x_{i_{3}}\exp\!\Big(\!\!-\!s^{\prime}\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+n(n-1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|x_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}x_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n(n-1)(n-2)}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+|\widetilde{x}_{3}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|x_{3}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{3}|+K_{2}x_{3})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}x_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|^{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|x_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+2|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+2x_{2})}x_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+|\widetilde{x}_{3}|)-aK_{2}(x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{3})}|\widetilde{x}_{1}||\widetilde{x}_{2}|x_{3}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{3}|+K_{2}x_{3})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{8}(s/n,3a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{4}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})A_{7}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})A_{3}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(III)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{(n-1)(n-2)}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})A_{4}^{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-3}\mathrm{d}s}_{(IV)}\bigg].

(F.75)

Here,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
,
A_{3}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
A_{4}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.3
, and
A_{7}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
A_{8}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
shares the same definition as in the proof in Lemma
F.8
.
Then, through similar procedures in the proof of Lemma
F.11
(by utilizing the Lipschitz continuities of these functions), we can derive that:

\displaystyle\bigg|(I)+\frac{A_{8}(0,3a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|(I)+\frac{3a\sigma^{4}K_{2}e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\big((1+9a^{2}K_{1}^{2}\sigma^{2})\Phi(-3aK_{1}\sigma)-3aK_{1}\sigma\phi(3aK_{1}\sigma)\big)}{2\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n};

\displaystyle\bigg|(II)+\frac{A_{4}(0,a,a,K_{1})A_{7}(0,2a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|(II)+\frac{a\sigma^{4}K_{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{\Phi^{3}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}\Big(\phi(2aK_{1}\sigma)-2aK_{1}\sigma\Phi(-2aK_{1}\sigma)\Big)\Big(\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{\pi}}e^{-\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{1}^{2}}{2}}+2aK_{1}\sigma\Phi(-aK_{1}\sigma)\Big)\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{n};

\displaystyle\bigg|(III)+\frac{A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})A_{3}(0,2a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg|(III)+a\sigma^{4}K_{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\frac{(1+4a^{2}K_{1}^{2}\sigma^{2})\Phi(-2aK_{1}\sigma)-2aK_{1}\sigma\phi(2aK_{1}\sigma)}{\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.76)

Specifically, for the term
(IV)
, we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|(IV)+\frac{A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})A_{4}^{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg|(IV)+\frac{a\sigma^{4}K_{2}}{2\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}\Big(\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{\pi}}e^{-\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}K_{1}^{2}}{2}}-2aK_{1}\sigma\Phi(-aK_{1}\sigma)\Big)^{2}\Bigg|\leq\frac{c_{4}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.77)

Lastly, by utilizing Taylor’s expansion regarding the function above w.r.t.
K_{1}
at
K_{1}=0
, and the facts that
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[K_{1}^{2}]=1/d
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbb{E}\big[K_{2}(IV)\big]+\frac{4}{\pi}a\sigma^{4}\Big|\leq\frac{c_{4}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{c_{5}(a,\sigma)}{d}.

(F.78)

Substituting the results of (
F.1
), (
F.1
), and (
F.78
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.18
.

Let

x_{1},x_{2},\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables, and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2},\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
i.i.d Gaussian random variables. In addition,
a
is a positive scalar, and
\bm{\theta}_{*},\bm{\theta}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are two independent random vectors following uniform
d-
dimensional sphere distribution, and we denote
K_{1}=\langle\bm{\theta}_{0},\bm{\theta}_{*}\rangle
,
K_{2}=\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\bm{\theta}_{*}\bm{\theta}_{*}^{\top})\bm{\theta}_{0}\|_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(2|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(2x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{2}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]+\frac{a\sigma^{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}}{n}\Bigg|\leq\frac{f_{33}(a,\sigma)}{n^{2}}+\frac{f_{34}(a,\sigma)}{nd}.

Here,
f_{33}(a,\sigma)
and
f_{34}(a,\sigma)
are both analytic functions of
a
and
\sigma
while irrelevant with
n
and
d
.

Proof of Lemma
F.18
.

Following a similar procedure in the proof of Lemma
F.12
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{K_{2}\sum_{i_{1}=1}^{n}\sum_{i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(2|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(2x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{2}}}{(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}})^{3}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}(2|\widetilde{x}_{i_{1}}|+|\widetilde{x}_{i_{2}}|)-aK_{2}(2x_{i_{1}}+x_{i_{2}})}x_{i_{2}}\exp\!\Big(\!-\!s^{\prime}\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|-aK_{2}x_{i}}\Big)\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}x_{1}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}(s^{\prime})^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(2|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(2x_{1}+x_{2})}x_{2}e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-s^{\prime}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-3aK_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|-3aK_{2}x_{1}}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}x_{1}|K_{1}\big]\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-aK_{1}(2|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+|\widetilde{x}_{2}|)-aK_{2}(2x_{1}+x_{2})}x_{2}e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{2}|+K_{2}x_{2})}}|K_{1}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\frac{s}{n}e^{-a(K_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{1}|+K_{2}x_{1})}}|K_{1}\big]\Big)^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{2}A_{2}(s/n,3a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-1}\mathrm{d}s}_{(I)}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{n-1}{2n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[K_{2}\underbrace{\int_{0}^{\infty}sA_{5}(s/n,2a,a,K_{1})A_{2}(s/n,a,a,K_{1})[M(s/n,a,K_{1})]^{n-2}\mathrm{d}s}_{(II)}\bigg],

(F.79)

Here,
A_{2}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
and
M(\lambda,a,K_{1})
share the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.1
, and
A_{5}(\lambda,\alpha,a,K_{1})
shares the same definitions as in the proof in Lemma
F.5
.
Then, through similar procedures in the proof of previous lemmas (by utilizing the Lipschitz continuities of these functions), we can derive that:

\displaystyle\bigg|(I)+\frac{A_{2}(0,3a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(I)+\frac{3a\sigma^{2}K_{2}e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-3aK_{1}\sigma)}{2\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{1}(a,\sigma)}{n},

(F.80)

and

\displaystyle\bigg|(II)+\frac{A_{5}(0,2a,a,K_{1})A_{2}(0,a,a,K_{1})}{4e^{3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi^{3}(-a\sigma K_{1})}\bigg|=\bigg|(II)+\frac{a\sigma^{2}K_{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-2aK_{1}\sigma)}{\Phi^{2}(-aK_{1}\sigma)}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{n}.

(F.81)

Specifically, by considering Taylor’s expansion at
K_{1}=0
of the function above, we can obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\mathbb{E}\big[K_{2}(II)\big]+2a\sigma^{2}e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\bigg|\leq\frac{c_{2}(a,\sigma)}{n}+\frac{c_{3}(a,\sigma)}{d}

(F.82)

Substituting the results of (
F.80
), (
F.81
), and (
F.82
) into (
F.1
), we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma F.19
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2}\ldots,x_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be
n
Gaussian random variables and
\widetilde{x}_{1},\widetilde{x}_{2}\ldots,\widetilde{x}_{n}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
be another
n
Gaussian random variables. In addition, let
a,b_{1},b_{2},k
be any absolute constants satisfying that
k
is an integer and
b_{1}^{2}+b_{2}^{2}=1
. Then for sufficiently large
n
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{k}e^{\frac{ka^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k}}\leq\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}e^{-a(b_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+b_{2}x_{i})}\Big)^{-k}\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{n^{k}e^{\frac{ka^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k}}+\frac{c_{a,\sigma,k}}{n^{k+1}},

where
c_{a,\sigma,k}=\frac{2\Phi(2|a|\sigma)}{n^{k+1}e^{(k-2)a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-|a|\sigma)^{k+2}}+1
is a constant solely depending on
a
,
\sigma
and
k
, and
\Phi(\cdot)
denotes the c.d.f. of standard Gaussian random variable.

Proof of Lemma
F.19
.

We first denote that
y_{i}=e^{-a(b_{1}|\widetilde{x}_{i}|+b_{2}x_{i})}
, and
S_{n}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}
. In addition, we can calculate that for any scalar
m
,
\mathbb{E}[y_{i}^{m}]=2e^{\frac{m^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-ma\sigma b_{1})
. For the lower bound, since the function
f(z)=z^{-k}
is convex for any
z\in(0,\infty)
, we utilize the Jensen’s inequality to directly derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[S_{n}^{-k}\big]\geq\big(\mathbb{E}[S_{n}]\big)^{-k}=\big(n\mathbb{E}[y_{1}]\big)^{-k}=\frac{1}{n^{k}e^{\frac{ka^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k}}.

(F.83)

To provide the upper bound, we leverage the Chernoff bound to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\Big)=\mathbb{P}\Big(e^{-\lambda S_{n}}\geq e^{-\lambda ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})}\Big)\leq\exp\!\big(n\varphi(\lambda)\big),

where
\lambda
is any positive scalar and
\varphi(\lambda)=\lambda e^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})+\log\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\lambda y_{i}}\big]
. By the fact that
\log(z)\leq z-1
and
e^{-z}\leq 1-z+\frac{z^{2}}{2}
for any
z\geq 0
, we can derive that

\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\lambda y_{i}}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\big[e^{-\lambda y_{i}}\big]-1\leq-\lambda\mathbb{E}[y_{i}]+\tfrac{\lambda^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}[y_{i}^{2}]=-2\lambda e^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})+\lambda^{2}e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-2a\sigma b_{1}),

which implies that
\varphi(\lambda)\leq-\lambda e^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})+\lambda^{2}e^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(-2a\sigma b_{1})
. By choosing
\lambda_{0}=\frac{e^{-3a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})}{2\Phi(-2a\sigma b_{1})}
, we have
\varphi(\lambda_{0})\leq-\frac{\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})^{2}}{4\Phi(-2a\sigma b_{1})}e^{-a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\leq-\frac{\Phi(-|a|\sigma)^{2}}{4\Phi(2|a|\sigma)}e^{-a^{2}\sigma^{2}}=-f(a,\sigma)
, and further result that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\Big)\leq e^{-f(a,\sigma)n}.

(F.84)

Now, we can start to derive the upper bound based on the established concentration results above. With Taylor’s expansion of
g(z)=z^{-k}
, we have

\displaystyle S_{n}^{-k}=\frac{1}{n^{k}e^{\frac{ka^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k}}-\frac{k\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)}{n^{k+1}e^{(k+1)a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k+1}}+\frac{k(k+1)\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}},

where
\xi_{n}
is a random variable between
S_{n}
and
2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})
. We take the expectation on both sides and derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[S_{n}^{-k}\big]=\frac{1}{n^{k}e^{\frac{ka^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}[2\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})]^{k}}+k(k+1)\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\bigg].

In the next, we separate the term
\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1}))^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\Big]
with the event
\{S_{n}\geq ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\bigg]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}\geq ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}\bigg].

We also consider providing the upper bounds for these two components, respectively. For the first term, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}\geq ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}]}{n^{k+2}e^{(k+2)a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-|a|\sigma)^{k+2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Phi(2|a|\sigma)}{n^{k+1}e^{(k-2)a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-|a|\sigma)^{k+2}},

(F.85)

where the first inequality holds if
\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}\geq ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}=1
, then
\xi_{n}
is also larger than
ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})
, and the second inequality holds as
\mathbb{E}[(S_{n}-2ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1}))^{2}]=n\mathrm{Var}(y_{i})\leq n\mathbb{E}[y_{1}^{2}]\leq 2ne^{2a^{2}\sigma^{2}}\Phi(2|a|\sigma)
. For the second term, we can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{\xi_{n}^{k+2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{2}}{S_{n}^{k+2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{\big(S_{n}-2ne^{\frac{a^{2}\sigma^{2}}{2}}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)^{4}}{S_{n}^{2k+4}}\bigg]^{1/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{P}\big(S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(S_{n}-2ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1}))^{8}]^{1/4}\mathbb{E}[y_{i}^{-4k-8}]^{1/4}e^{-f(a,\sigma)n}\leq\frac{1}{k(k+1)n^{k+1}}.

(F.86)

Here, the first inequality holds as
\xi_{n}>S_{n}
if
S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})
. The second and third inequalities are both derived by Cauchy-Schwarz’s inequality, the facts that
S_{n}^{-k}\leq y_{1}^{-k}
, and

\mathbb{P}\big(S_{n}<ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1})\big)\leq e^{-f(a,\sigma)n}
derived in (
F.84
). The last inequality holds as the
\mathbb{E}[(S_{n}-2ne^{a^{2}\sigma^{2}/2}\Phi(-a\sigma b_{1}))^{8}]\leq c^{\prime}_{a,\sigma,k}n^{4}
by Rosenthal’s inequality
(
46
)
, and
\mathbb{E}[y_{1}^{-4k-8}]^{1/4}\leq 2e^{2(k+2)^{2}a^{2}\sigma^{2}}
. Combining the results of (
F.1
) and (
F.1
), we finish the proof for the upper bound.
∎

F.2
Properties of Gaussian random variables, uniform sphere random variables, log-concave random variables, and covering number on unit sphere

Lemma F.20
.

Let
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}
be two independent random vectors, each distributed uniformly on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
(that is, each is a unit random vector with the rotation-invariant probability measure). Let
K=\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle
. Then its probability density function is

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(K\leq k):=f_{K}(k)=\frac{\Gamma\!\big(\tfrac{d}{2}\big)}{\sqrt{\pi}\,\Gamma\!\big(\tfrac{d-1}{2}\big)}\,(1-k^{2})^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{-1\leq k\leq 1\}}.

In addition,
K
is independent with
\mathbf{a}
, and
\mathbf{b}
respectively. Moreover
\mathbb{E}[K]=0
,
\mathrm{Var}(K)=1/d
, and
\mathbb{E}[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}^{\top})\mathbf{b}\|_{2}]=\frac{\Gamma^{2}(\frac{d}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{d-1}{2})\Gamma(\frac{d+1}{2})}
.

Proof of Lemma
F.20
.

Since
\mathbf{a}
and
\mathbf{b}
are rotation invariant, implying that for any orthogonal matrix
\mathbf{R}
, we have
\mathbf{R}\mathbf{a}
and
\mathbf{R}\mathbf{b}
still following the unit sphere distribution. Consequently, we can always find a specific
\mathbf{R}
such that
\mathbf{R}\mathbf{a}=\mathbf{e}_{1}
. Then we have
K=\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle=\langle\mathbf{R}\mathbf{a},\mathbf{R}\mathbf{b}\rangle=(\mathbf{R}\mathbf{b})_{1}
, the first coordinate of a unit sphere random vector. And it is evident that this random variable would be independent with
\mathbf{a}
, and similarly also independent with
\mathbf{b}
. In the next we derive the p.d.f. of
K
. We have shown that
K
has the same distribution as
\mathbf{a}_{1}
. In addition, the differential w.r.t. the polar coordinate system indicates that
\mathrm{d}S=\sin^{d-2}(\alpha_{1})\sin^{d-3}(\alpha_{2})\cdots\sin(\phi_{d-2})\mathrm{d}\alpha_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}\alpha_{d-2}
, where
S
is the area of unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\alpha_{1},\ldots,\alpha_{d-1}
are the angles of the polar coordinate system. Since
\mathbf{a}_{1}=\cos\alpha_{1}
, and the marginal density function of
\alpha_{1}
is proportional to
\sin^{d-2}(\alpha_{1})
, we have

\displaystyle f_{K}(k)\propto\sin^{d-2}(\alpha_{1})\frac{1}{\sin(\alpha_{1})}=(1-k^{2})^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{-1\leq k\leq 1\}}.

In addition, we have
\int_{-1}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\mathrm{d}t=2\int_{0}^{1}(1-t^{2})^{\frac{d-3}{2}}\mathrm{d}t=\mathrm{Beta}\big(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{d-1}{2}\big)
, which proves the p.d.f.
of
K
. By symmetry,
f_{K}
is an even function, hence
\mathbb{E}[K]=0
. To compute
\mathbb{E}[K^{2}]
, one can use either the density or a coordinate argument. Using coordinates, write
\mathbf{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{d})
and
\mathbf{b}=(b_{1},\dots,b_{d})
. By independence and symmetry of the uniform spherical law,

\mathbb{E}[K^{2}]=\mathbb{E}\big[\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i,j}a_{i}a_{j}b_{i}b_{j}\Big]=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}]\ \mathbb{E}[b_{i}b_{j}].

For a uniform unit vector on
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
we have
\mathbb{E}[a_{i}a_{j}]=\frac{1}{d}\delta_{ij}
. Hence

\mathbb{E}[K^{2}]=\sum_{i,j}\frac{1}{d}\delta_{ij}\cdot\frac{1}{d}\delta_{ij}=\sum_{i}\frac{1}{d^{2}}=\frac{1}{d}.

Thus
\operatorname{Var}(K)=\mathbb{E}[K^{2}]-(\mathbb{E}[K])^{2}=1/d
. In addition, we can calculate that

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}^{\top})\mathbf{b}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\mathbf{b}\|_{2}^{2}-2\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}+\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}\|\mathbf{a}\|_{2}^{2}=1-\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}.

Then by the density function of
K=\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle
demonstrated previously, we can easily derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|(\mathbf{I}_{d}-\mathbf{a}\mathbf{a}^{\top})\mathbf{b}\|_{2}]=\mathbb{E}[\sqrt{1-\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}\rangle^{2}}]=\frac{\Gamma^{2}(\frac{d}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{d-1}{2})\Gamma(\frac{d+1}{2})}.

In addition, by Gautschi’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma^{2}(\frac{d}{2})}{\Gamma(\frac{d-1}{2})\Gamma(\frac{d+1}{2})}=\frac{\Gamma^{2}(\frac{d}{2})}{\frac{d-1}{2}\Gamma^{2}(\frac{d-1}{2})}\geq\frac{\frac{d-1}{2}}{d/2}=1-\frac{1}{d}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Lemma F.21
.

Let
\mathbf{a}
be a random vector distributed uniformly on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a standard Gaussian random vector. Then it holds that
\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}\rangle
is independent with
\mathbf{a}
.

Proof of Lemma
F.21
.

It is evident that for any fixed
\mathbf{a}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
, the conditional distribution
\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}\rangle|\mathbf{a}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Since this conditional distribution does not depend on the specific choice of
\mathbf{a}
, the random variable
\langle\mathbf{a},\mathbf{x}\rangle
is independent of the random vector
\mathbf{a}
. This completes the proof
∎

Lemma F.22
.

Let
x_{1},x_{2}
be two Gaussian random variables with zero mean, and
y=\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(x_{1})
. Then
y
is independent with
y\cdot x_{1}
and
y\cdot x_{2}
. Moreover,
y\cdot x_{2}
also follows the normal distribution, which has zero mean and the same variance with
x_{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
F.22
.

W.L.O.G., we assume that
x_{1},x_{2}
are standard Gaussian random variables. We first prove that
y
is independent with
y\cdot x_{1}
. It is clear that
y\cdot x_{1}=|x_{1}|
, which is independent with
y
.
Next, we prove that
y
is independent with
y\cdot x_{2}
and
y\cdot x_{2}
follows the standard normal distribution. If
y=1
then
y\cdot x_{2}=x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. On the other hand when
y=-1
,
y\cdot x_{2}=-x_{2}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Therefore,
y\cdot x_{2}|y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Since this conditional distribution does not depend on
y
, we have
y\cdot x_{2}
is independent with
y
, and follows the standard Gaussian distribution. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma F.23

(Lemma 2 in
6
)
.

Suppose that
a
is a one-dimensional isotropic log-concave random variable, with
f_{a}(x)
denoting its probability density function, then
f_{a}(x)\leq 1
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma F.24

(Lemma 3 and Theorem 4 in
6
)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
are two
d
-dimensional unit vectors. In addition, let
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a random vector generated from an isotropic log-concave distribution, then there exist two absolute positive constants
c_{-}\leq c_{+}
such that

\displaystyle c_{-}\cdot\angle(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\mathbf{u},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\neq\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\langle\mathbf{v},\mathbf{x}\rangle)\big)\leq c_{+}\cdot\angle(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}),

where
\angle(\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v})
denotes the angle between unit vectors
\mathbf{u}
and
\mathbf{v}
.

Lemma F.25

(Paouris’ inequality)
.

Suppose that
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
follows
d
-dimensional isotropic log-concave distribution, then for any
s>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\geq cs\sqrt{d}\big)\leq e^{-s\sqrt{d}},

where
c
is an absolute positive constant.

Lemma F.26

(Lemma 5.2 in
54
)
.

Let
N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)
denotes the
\epsilon
-net on unit Euclidean sphere
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
equipped
with the Euclidean metric. Then it holds that for any
\epsilon>0
:

\displaystyle\big|N(\mathbb{S}^{d-1},\epsilon)\big|\leq\bigg(1+\frac{2}{\epsilon}\bigg)^{d}.

The proofs of Lemmas
F.23
and
F.24
can be found in
6
, the proof of Lemma
F.25
can be found in
1
, and the proof of Lemma
F.26
can be found in
54
.
